<a id="shelves-lifespeedlight"></a>

### New: Life at the Speed of Light: From the Double Helix to the Dawn of Digital Life — J. Craig Venter

Eligible first-hand account of research and synthetic-genome engineering, not a technical curriculum or nutrition advice. The source reading report explicitly criticizes its teleportation analogy; a mention is not approval. No high-confidence veto established: documented experimental work is substantial, while future scenarios are speculative and acknowledged cellular-context limits constrain the DNA/software metaphor. Do not treat the book as proof that DNA alone is sufficient for life. T11=T12=0; biological engineering is central. Mandatory coverage, not selected for the praised 200 on the mixed reader evidence.

Status: recommended. Verdict: Low priority; confidence moderate. 

| Criterion | Linear attainment | Contribution | Assessment |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| E | 14.00 / 20 | 9.80 | First-hand experimental access is valuable; sweeping analogies and self-promotional framing lower evidential confidence outside the documented research. |
| S | 11.00 / 17 | 7.12 | Connects sequencing, synthesis, computation and laboratory organization, with thinner economic or institutional analysis. |
| U | 10.00 / 15 | 10.00 | Research bottlenecks and iterative troubleshooting offer reusable lessons. |
| M | 9.00 / 12 | 9.00 | Concrete engineering of genomes is explained; the pre-existing cellular machinery is essential and must not disappear from the model. |
| D | 7.00 / 10 | 4.90 | D=7/10, moderate confidence from contents, Karpathy's reading report and professional reviews. Reading, writing and transplanting genomes add distinct learning. Futuristic analogies and autobiographical emphasis can dilute the experimental account. |
| R | 6.00 / 9 | 6.00 | Experimental history lasts, while forecasts and contemporary frontier claims age quickly. |
| F | 7.00 / 7 | 7.00 | Strong fit for biological engineering, information processing and invention. |
| A | 4.50 / 5 | 4.50 | Leading participant with direct expertise, balanced against his personal stake in the narrative. |
| N | 2.00 / 3 | 2.00 | Research chronology is useful, but movement into speculative scenarios weakens the structure. |
| P | 1.00 / 2 | 1.00 | Software and teleportation metaphors can obscure necessary physical conditions. |

<a id="shelves-rationalityaizombies"></a>

### New: Rationality: From AI to Zombies — Eliezer Yudkowsky

Mandatory Karpathy coverage, with Subcritical and McAteer occurrences merged once each. Published ebook compilation, with component print volumes verified by MIRI; not an unpublished blog reference. General epistemology and cognitive-bias explanation, not a specialist technical curriculum. Religious criticism and personal motivation are parts rather than the dominant purpose, so V7/V8 do not automatically apply. Miller praises useful reasoning material and discloses support for MIRI; Goertzel challenges the AI-risk framework. That disagreement does not by itself establish a veto on this broad compilation's central reasoning content. Do not treat contested quantum interpretation or AI claims as settled. Philosophy central, T12=-10. Not selected: very long, unevenly edited and disputed specialist applications.

Status: recommended. Verdict: Poor fit; confidence moderate. 

| Criterion | Linear attainment | Contribution | Assessment |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| E | 12.00 / 20 | 7.20 | Useful probability reasoning mixed with confident extrapolations and psychological findings needing current verification. |
| S | 9.00 / 17 | 4.76 | Connects cognition, decision theory, evolution and AI, less strongly to real institutions. |
| U | 11.00 / 15 | 11.00 | Many reusable questions about evidence and belief revision; practical effectiveness is not uniformly demonstrated. |
| M | 7.00 / 12 | 7.00 | Explains some bias mechanisms but evolutionary and AI extrapolations remain speculative. |
| D | 5.00 / 10 | 2.50 | D=5, moderate confidence from sampled introduction, contents, Miller and detailed readers. Many distinctions add learning; blog-derived repetition, digressions and personal narrative reduce whole-book yield. |
| R | 7.00 / 9 | 7.00 | Reasoning principles endure; empirical examples and AI assumptions need revision. |
| F | 5.00 / 7 | 5.00 | Computation and heuristics fit well; elementary physics has limited added value for this reader. |
| A | 3.00 / 5 | 3.00 | Influential independent AI writer, with sympathetic and critical specialist engagement. |
| N | 2.00 / 3 | 2.00 | Short essays are accessible individually; the compilation is sprawling. |
| P | 1.00 / 2 | 1.00 | Memorable formulations coexist with rhetorical overconfidence. |

<a id="shelves-autonomy"></a>

### New: Autonomy: The Quest to Build the Driverless Car—and How It Will Reshape Our World — Lawrence D. Burns; Christopher Shulgan

Selected for technology-industry review praise and Goodreads 4.13/952. IEEE Spectrum's Mark Harris praises the engineering narrative but identifies Waymo loyalty and omitted security, congestion and social risks. Transportation writer Edward Niedermeyer also distinguishes the valuable development history from optimistic forecasts. This is a narrative of engineering and organizational change, not technical training. Its historical value does not depend primarily on forecasts coming true, so no V4 veto is established; forecasts are not treated as current facts. Technical production dominates over politics or environmental advocacy. US development has global technological influence, so thematic deductions are zero.

Status: recommended. Verdict: Selective; confidence moderate. 

| Criterion | Linear attainment | Contribution | Assessment |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| E | 14.00 / 20 | 9.80 | Insider access supports the development history; company ties and omitted risks weaken the broader forecasts. |
| S | 14.00 / 17 | 11.53 | Links sensors, software, automotive engineering, research competitions, business models and regulation. |
| U | 11.00 / 15 | 11.00 | Useful contrasts between established production organizations and experimental development teams. |
| M | 9.00 / 12 | 9.00 | Explains development obstacles and organizational choices, though safety validation receives less depth. |
| D | 7.50 / 10 | 5.63 | D=7.5, moderate confidence from Harris, Niedermeyer, contents and reader reports. DARPA trials and later deployment add different constraints; visionary passages provide less established learning. |
| R | 6.00 / 9 | 6.00 | Engineering history will last; adoption and economic forecasts are less durable. |
| F | 7.00 / 7 | 7.00 | Direct fit to computing, engineering, transport and technological change. |
| A | 4.50 / 5 | 4.50 | Author's automotive R&D experience offers access, with a disclosed commercial perspective. |
| N | 3.00 / 3 | 3.00 | Clear narrative progression makes a technically complex development readable. |
| P | 1.50 / 2 | 1.50 | Accessible prose sometimes becomes promotional. |
